ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u12	Thank you, sir. And Mrs Henryka Krzywonos-Strycharska, please take the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u13	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate's draft law amending the Act on anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons and some other laws contains, in principle, positive provisions that correct the existing state of affairs, but also contains worrying changes that this law is about to introduce. It is positive to correct the rules already in force that anti-communist opposition activists from the vending machine will receive an unlimited allowance, not for a period of usually 1 year, if they fulfil many factors. They will therefore be treated autonomously, not as if they were going to apply for social allowances. Communication, health, cultural, educational and housing facilities also have a positive reception. However, it should be pointed out that the introduction of facilities will be imposed on local governments and there are no provisions to provide additional funding for these purposes. According to the Act, the legitimacy and badge of the opposition activist will be granted ex officio after granting the status of opposition activist. This provision will eliminate unnecessary bureaucracy because opposition activists will not have to submit a separate proposal. It is only a pity that the Senate rejected with the votes of the Senators the offer of half of the benefit after the death of the person entitled to the spouses, who often incurred the costs of the opposition activity of the husband or wife. However, the provisions concerning the election of the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons are worrying. The Head shall be appointed, in accordance with the proposed provisions, by the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister responsible for social security. Thus, the open competition selection is discontinued. The location of voivodship consultative councils on opposition activists and repressed persons is also doubtful. These councils are to act, in accordance with the proposed regulations, by the voivodships and not, as so far, by the management boards of the voivodships. The above-mentioned solutions raise doubts as to whether the chief and council working at the voivodships will be 100% Apolitical and fair in their actions. In conclusion, the proposed law is heading in the right direction in terms of solutions to help opposition activists in the field of aid and finance. However, there is great doubt as to whether all opposition activists will be treated equally, regardless of the political views currently presented, and whether it will not be so that the opponent will only be the one who shares the views of the current rulers. However, I hope that we will finally respect the people through whom we live today in a free country, which is part of the European Union and NATO, and without further discussion we will adopt this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u14	Thank you very much, Member. And please take the floor of Mr Józef Brynkus.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u15	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the draft law amending the Act on anti-communist opposition activists in print no. 936. Ladies and gentlemen, communism destroyed Polish society materially, physically and mentally, so only some had the courage to openly oppose it. Prisons, expulsions and schools, forced military service, torture during interrogations, and brutal beatings, even ending in death, met them. And yet the opposition's actions, often lasting many years, led to the collapse of communist power. It would seem, then, that those who have taken credit in the fight against the criminal system in free Poland will be appreciated. Sadly, it was only over a quarter of a century after we regained our freedom that we remembered those to whom we owe our freedom. We have recalled in a far insufficient way, as evidenced by the flawed, imperfect and already at the time of the adoption of the criticism of the 2015 Act. It offers alms to those to whom we owe our freedom by imposing the need to apply for it. Meanwhile, communist abusers and cameramen still get high apanasia. These errors should be corrected as soon as possible. We have therefore called in recent years for compensation for the harm suffered by anti-communist opposition activists. I myself requested this in my first interpellation to the Polish government, demanding the reaction of the authorities of the Polish state to the difficult situation of life and material people deserved for Polish freedom. With regard to the proposed amendments, I would like to draw the attention of the High House to the following elements. The extension of the anti-communist period defined in the Act of 4 June 1989 until 31 July 1990 is fully justified. It was then that the Security Service was formally wound up until this point leading to permanent action against opponents - and today its former officers raise their heads hard. A spectacular example of criminal action against opponents is murder on Fr. Sylvester of Zichu, which took place after the elections on 4 June 1989. The granting of permanent witnessing to every opposition activist is a minimal and symbolic gesture of assistance and compensation for the harm he has suffered. This will eliminate the embarrassing issue of begging poor independence activists with penniless alms proposed in the current law. However, it is a pity that it is only 400 PLN. I believe that this benefit should be higher. Let me remind you that the beneficiaries of the communist system for several decades received amounts many times exceeding these symbolic 400 PLN. We cannot explain ourselves in modest ways. After all, the governing coalition constantly emphasizes the excellent state of the budget. I remind you that after Poland regained its independence in 1918, Józef Piłsudski carried out a bill in the poor and struggling with the non-election remnants of Poland under which the state gave life to former insurgents in January. Today, when Poland is in a much better economic position, the appreciation of anti-communist opposition activists should be our priority and no cost calculations should obscure this priority. One further reservation should be made, resulting from the inability of many former opponents who often struggle with bailouts. This benefit should be compulsoryly excluded from the bail-in and not included in the amount exempt from that occupation. A separate issue is the granting of special pensions to those living in difficult conditions to opposition activists and those who are being repressed. After 1989, it was not verified that such pensions were paid and only granted to people who were often associated with post-communist or circular governing teams. Undoubtedly, this form of support should be extended to those who require such assistance, in fact, deserved for Poland, which are activists of the anti-communist opposition and those who are being repressed for anti-system activities in the Polish People's Republic. Changes to the Veterans Act are also fully justified; e.g. The extension of the period of activity until 1963 seems obvious in the context of the death this year of the last soldier cursed Józef Franczak, a doll. To sum up, Kukiz15 is in favour of adopting the proposed law with the hope of immediate, true and full appreciation of anti-communist opposition activists, thanks to which we live in free Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u21	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! Opposite activists, I welcome you very cordially! The debate on the state's recognition of the merits of those who were fighting for Poland's independence began in 2000, but then the tone of this debate was given by the Gazeta Wyborcza, in which Adam Michnik wrote: "We are not veterans of solidarity, and we do not need any help, because our environment has settled well. I think he told the truth here, because the environment associated with the Gazeta Wyborcza is very well placed in the Third Republic of Poland. We know very well the mechanisms of this setting, so I won't talk about it. The Act on anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons entered into force in the fourth quarter of 2015. The entire legislative process concerning the Act lasted for years and the final effect was far from preliminary intentions. However, the previous coalition and parliament did not change much their perception of those who fought the communist dictatorship. She could not agree to the proposals of the anti-communist opposition and the oppressed. The lack of sufficient reflection and research on the life situation of members and activists of the anti-communist opposition, members of their families, as well as ordinary citizens subjected to repression, including material ones, has led Parliament to adopt a solution based on an over-precise rule. This is a delicate term because the current law limits the possibility of granting benefits in principle only for a period of one year, taking into account the relevant income thresholds. At present, benefits are social assistance. The proposed bill meets the expectations of a large group of activists of the widely understood anti-communist opposition. In addition to changes in the granting of assistance to opposition activists and repressed persons, it amends and clarifies the definition of the person being repressed. It is also particularly important to abolish the social nature of aid to opposition activists and oppressed persons. The proposed amendment provides for the autonomous nature of the benefit granted, as stated in the Veteran Act. The scope and amount of benefits are probably not fully satisfactory, but are not compensation for the damage and losses suffered. They are a form of recognition of the status of those who fought for free Poland, regardless of the threat of repression. That's a plus and a huge advantage of the presented project. However, it cannot be accepted that those who obtain the status of anti-communist opposition activist or repressed person, after receiving the proposed financial supplement, do not have a minimum of income guaranteed at the level that we provide in the de-ubecisation law to their Catholics, i.e. at least PLN 2050 gross. I believe it is unacceptable that the oppressed do not have a minimum income guaranteed in the Act. I am talking about people who, after taking into account the additional benefits provided for in the Act in extreme cases, will receive only PLN 1100 if they have a pension, or PLN 1400 if they are retired. It's inhuman. It can't be that the executioners are still better off than the victims. I know there may not be a lot of people like that, but there are. I personally know former workers living in poverty for less than PLN 1000. It is precisely in the case of such people that we should increase the benefits to PLN 2100, i.e. to such an amount, that after receiving them the income of the oppressed persons should be increased to PLN 2100. Although symbolically they have exceeded the income guaranteed by ubekom. I hope that the absence of such a guarantee is merely an oversight of the formally prepared proposal, which is why I am making an amendment to the bill, which will correct the omission. I hope that the amendment will go to the committee and that the bill will be adopted without delay. From this point on behalf of myself and the Free and Solidarnia wheels, I would like to express my special appreciation for all those who have spared no effort in fighting for free Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u55	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law on the amendment of the Act on the National Judicial Council and some other laws really arouses great political emotions, arouses emotions in the judicial environments, in the legal environments. This is completely understandable because these environments are interested in maintaining the existing situation as regards the judiciary. In particular, it is worth noting that the National Judicial Council is not a judicial authority. It's worth noting for starters. In accordance with the Article. The 175th Constitution of Justice in the Republic of Poland is governed by the Supreme Court, common courts, administrative courts and military courts. Therefore, it is clear from this provision that the National Judicial Council is not a judicial authority. It is also important to stress that it is the ordinary legislator who has been empowered by the Constitution to define the system, scope and mode of work of the National Courts Council, as well as the way in which its members are selected. This is expressly provided for in Article. 187 constitutions. This is indeed a far-reaching constitutional right. I don't think I've come across another place of the constitution to allow the constitution to define the body of the National Judicial Council. Here we are dealing with this authority to determine the system. Using this mandate, the project introduces a new system of the National Judicial Council, creating a first assembly and a second assembly. Why does that serve? It must be said that under the Constitution, the National Judicial Council has very important competences. She is the only authority that can apply to the President for a judge's nomination at all levels of the judiciary. It's a far-reaching authority, no other authority has such powers. At the same time, the majority - one can even say that a very large majority - have judges in the National Courts Council. On 25 members of the National Judicial Board, 15 of them are judges plus the first President of the Supreme Court and the President of the Supreme Administrative Court. So, if we look at this, there are 17 judges on the 25 members of the National Judicial Council. The remaining members are 4 Members, 2 Senators, Minister for Justice. It follows that this remaining group of members is in a definite minority, their voice simply does not matter and does not matter at all. The judges have the power to vote every resolution on the National Judicial Council. I don't think that's what the constitution provided for. The idea was that, in fact, a consensus should be drawn up within the National Council of Courts, and some agreement should be drawn up. Hence representatives of various institutions. This is not the case, however, because since it has been realized that the judges have the majority, they impose a tone on the work. So in a simple way, it can be said that in Poland, the judges decide who will become a judge. Thus nowhere in Europe in the cases I know is. In particular, it is worth recalling that in some European countries, there is no National Judicial Council and no one there says that democracy is at risk for this. No one's saying that the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges are at stake. This is not enough, in some countries, in particular Austria, [[♪ The bell ♪]] in Germany, the Federal Minister of Justice, in the Netherlands, the Minister of Justice, has applied for the appointment of a judge, and there no one raises the threshold that the legislative authority and executive authority are interfering with the justice system. In Poland, great powers of the judicial community and a continuous charge of interference by the legislative authority and the executive authority. It is only worth recalling in password that according to the article. The 4th constitution is a nation's sovereign power. If the nation can fulfil this function through its representatives with regard to the first two authorities, then there is no mechanism with regard to the third power, to the justice system. There is no instrument so far that the nation can exercise its sovereignty. This situation needs to be changed and this project changes this situation.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u99	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, As ombudsman, I felt that the Act on the National Judicial Council was of fundamental importance from the point of view of respect for civil rights and freedoms. It is difficult to imagine that without an independent court our rights and freedoms will be protected: both if the citizen is in an indictment situation, if the citizen is in a situation where the court is the guarantor, that the prosecutor will not exceed his powers to take various procedural actions in the preparatory proceedings and in a situation of dispute with the State, e.g. property disputes with the State Treasury or with companies of the State Treasury, or when cases are resolved which may have consequences for the state budget. These situations when a citizen comes into contact with the apparatus of the state and when he is counting on independent judges, there is a lot and the fact that the courts are independent and the judges are free to decide such matters is the foundation of our democracy, precisely our freedom. Therefore, the Law on the National Judicial Council, as well as the Law on the system of universal courts to be held, are of fundamental importance from this point of view, because if we touch such sensitive matter as the independence of courts from other types of authorities, and especially from the executive authority, then we must be aware of the consequences of the threat to citizens. As ombudsman, I presented two opinions on the Act on the National Judicial Council. Director Wróblewski from my office was honoured to present his first opinion in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. I would like to address two of the most important points. The draft law on the National Judicial Council makes this fundamental change, I would even say that there is a breach of the thin red line, what was just established at the round table as a result of long deliberations, which was patronized by Prof. Adam Strzembosz, because the principle of appointment of the judges of the National Judicial Council is changing completely. Our system, our constitution, is based on the fact that it is the National Judicial Council that combines elements, one can say, political, consisting in representation of the Sejm and the Senate, representation of the President and representation of the judicial community by appointing either the first President of the Supreme Court, or the President of the NSA, or 15 members of judges of the National Judicial Council. And it doesn't matter who appoints those members of the National Judicial Council, it doesn't matter that this appointment comes from the judges themselves. If we look at international standards that have been expressed in various kinds of documents, among others. In the Kiev rules on judicial independence adopted by the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe, it is clearly stated that most members of the judicial bodies should be appointed by the judges themselves - the word "peers" is used - i.e. by persons who are equal to those appointed. In this context, the shift in this burden of appointment to the Sejm is a change which does not meet international standards, as the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe has put forward in a very comprehensive opinion. The context of Spain is very often invoked in the Polish debate. It is said that in Spain, the parliament appoints members of the national judicial council, but at the same time it is not seen that Spain is being criticised by, for example, a body such as GRECO, a body of the Council of Europe, which is dealing with the fight against corruption. The same body that the Minister of Justice referred to when he demanded that judges make public declarations of property. It was in Greco's recommendations that it appeared. At the same time, it is not recognised that in the same Spain a majority of 3/5 of the parliament is required to elect those judges of the judicial council. Nor is it recognised that the list of candidates is forwarded to Parliament by the General Council of the Court of Justice, and this is the list that is being voted on. So, in our context, it would have to be in such a way that the candidates are first submitted, the candidates are elected by the National Judicial Council and only Parliament is voting. However, my goal is not to analyse the Spanish solutions or to analyse the solutions applied in other countries, Belgium, Croatia, France, Hungary, Italy, the Netherlands, because in the OSCE's opinion we can read this very carefully on p. 18 and p. 19. On the other hand, I protest against some kind of intellectual dishonesty. We cannot, by proposing such solutions, choose only those that fit us, forgetting what the general context is, what is the political culture in a country and what these solutions serve, how they have been developed over the years. The context and political culture are fundamental. That is why such solutions are created, not other ones, in order to protect the independence of such institutions. This intellectual dishonesty, in my opinion, is unacceptable, especially if it is done by people who are lecturers of the Faculty of Law and Administration at the University of Warsaw. Ladies and gentlemen! One of the demands of the Act is to shorten the term of office of the members of the National Judicial Council. To be honest, when I read the bill, I could not believe that the legislator was at all able to propose something like this, that changes in the functioning of constitutional bodies are envisaged, while assuming that the term of office would be shortened immediately after these changes were adopted. Here someone might ask himself a rhetorical question: Who will be next? Will the next one be the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber, whose legislature will shorten the term of office, because it is also a constitutional term, or maybe the Ombudsman, whose term can be terminated by a law adopted by a parliamentary majority? This is contrary to the principles of parliamentary art, contrary to the principles of the democratic rule of law, that such actions are carried out, that the term of office is reduced by the decision of the Sejm. If any changes are made, they should be preceded by a deep reflection and should then be adopted and refer to a possible future term of office. This is the standard of the democratic state. High Izbo! I believe that the justice system needs to be changed. I see this in complaints that are addressed to the Ombudsman's office, and I know this from my many years of practice as a human rights officer. I see various types of irregularities in the functioning of the justice system. The question is whether the system solutions that are proposed are the right way. Why are we not talking about reducing the cognition of courts, the involvement of the social factor in the administration of justice, the involvement of judicial experts, how to increase the participation of alternative dispute resolution methods such as mediation, how to strengthen organisationally courts, how to equip judges with more assistants and referenda? I could multiply a variety of topics that require reflection and reflection. Instead, a change in the system is proposed, a change which undermines the guarantee of independence of judges and the independence of the judiciary. I would like to stress that the adoption of the law on the National Council of Courts and the fact that politicians will have a decisive influence on the cast of the National Council of Courts, not the judges themselves, will cause the spine of an independent judiciary to be broken in Poland. There will be a break with our political tradition, which has been established within the framework of the round table. The National Judicial Council will have an impact on who can become a judge and will consequently have an impact on the functioning of independent courts. This court will then decide on various matters that concern our rights and freedoms. Unfortunately, as an ombudsman, I fear that the absence of an independent court may have negative consequences for the protection of rights and freedoms. There were words here about the judge. Because if we think about all the laws that have been adopted or that may be adopted in the near future, unfortunately, they lead to such changes. I therefore call for reflection, and I believe that the Act in its present form, as I have put forward in previous opinions, should not be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The Commission, at its meeting on 2 June 2017, considered the report of the Emergency Sub-Committee of 30 May 2017 and amended 14 amendments to the original draft. First of all, I would like to remind you of the most important changes to this bill. That's, among other things. a change concerning the authorisation of the Minister of Justice to the Secretaries and Undersecretaries of State to delegate the powers of the Minister, the appointment by the Minister of Justice of the Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Courts without the opinion of the Judges' Assembly, the establishment of a procedure for the dismissal of the Presidents of the Courts in accordance with the conditions laid down in the draft, the new rules for the appointment of judges of Visitors and a new model of visits, the detailed definition of the legal matter contained in the Rules of Procedure of the Courts, the procedure for making the file available and for presenting them to the Minister of Justice, the assessment of the qualifications of candidates for the Judge, the rights and the duties of the Coordinators for Justice. International cooperation in criminal and civil matters, the transfer of a judge to another department, the introduction of new tools for external and internal supervision of the administrative activities of the courts in the form of an assessment of the annual information submitted by the Presidents of the courts at all levels and in the form of a reference to administrative irregularities, the introduction of an institution for rehabilitation leave of a judge as a period during which it will be possible to restore the full capacity of the judge to perform his duties, the departure from the existing system of evaluation of the judge's work and professional development planning, the acquisition of the right to rest by judges of women, and prosecutors of women of age corresponding to the general retirement age, i.e. 60 years, introducing the possibility of a delegation of judges, including to the Registry of the President of the Republic of Poland and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, detailing the catalogue of information in the statements of property of judges and prosecutors, as well as a delegation to the Minister of Justice concerning the issuing of a regulation on the model form for a declaration of property statement, granting the powers of the Treasury arising from copyright rights to computer programs by decision of the Minister of Justice, setting up the Justice Fund as an instrument of assistance for victims and witnesses, regulating the conditions for the application for return to the position of judge, introducing as a rule the system of the random allocation of cases to judges and the principle of equal burdening judges with cases to ensure equal and fair burden on judicial staff and guaranteeing impartiality to parties for proceedings, amending the Act on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund enabling the transfer of retired judges and prosecutors - women at the age of 60 and men at the age of 65 - the possibility of appeal of the current President of the courts by the Minister of Justice within 6 months of the entry into force of the Act. The Commission of Justice, as I have already stated, introduced 14 amendments at its meeting on 2 June 2017. I'd like to zoom in on the most important of them. Amendment concerning Article. 9aa on the powers of the Minister of Justice which may be entrusted to the Secretaries or Undersecretaries of State in the Ministry of Justice, with the exception of the powers to issue a decision to transfer the Judge without his consent, i.e. except for the existing Article. 75 § 2 Law on the system of common courts. Transfer of the judge to another department without his consent in the event that no other judge in that department has agreed to the transfer, then the criterion of transfer will be the traineeship of the judges. As a rule, a judge with the lowest judicial internship will be transferred. A binding opinion of the National Judicial Council as a constitutional body protecting the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges was introduced in the appeal procedure when the National Judicial Council adopted a resolution on this matter by a majority of 2/3 of its votes. The provisions of the Law on the System of General Courts have been adapted to the amending provisions of the Act of 11 May 2017 amending the Act on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office in the field of judicial assessors, referenda, statutory authorisation to issue a regulation by the Minister of Justice laying down the Rules of Procedure for the Office of the Courts, modification of the model of access to the judicial profession. The next amendment was supplemented by guidelines for issuing a Regulation of the Minister of Justice on the principles of random allocation of cases. The principle was introduced that a judge entitled to retire on any given day, but with a three-month notice, should notify the President of the Court of Justice of the exercise of that right, which should have a positive effect on the organisation of the court's work. New conditions have been introduced for the appointment of an assistant prosecutor of the prosecutor's office. This applies to persons who have passed a judicial or bar exam and for at least 3 years have carried out activities related to the creation or application of law in offices serving public authorities or have a doctorate degree in legal sciences. The article has been deleted. 14 as a transitional provision and introduced as an adjustment provision in the proposed law. It concerns information contained in the statement of property of judges made before 6 January 2017 as at 31 December 2016, which will be made available in the Public Information Bulletin within 2 months of the date of entry into force of the Act. The vacathio legis of this law has also been amended. The original draft included a calendar date on 1 July 2017, currently a minimum period of 14 days for the entry into force of this law, with the exception of Article. 13, which will enter into force on 1 October 2017. I'll remind you, Art. 13 concerns the Pension Act. High Izbo! I would like to thank you very much for working in the extraordinary subcommittee, the fruit of which was these 14 amendments, and for working in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, and I request that the High House adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u115	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft law on common courts is undoubtedly unprecedented, in a negative sense, because it significantly overturns the constitutional order based on the classic threefold division of power. The scope of violations of the Polish Constitution is so huge that the project has been widely criticised both in Poland and abroad. The most dangerous solution must certainly be the granting of new competences to the executive authority which have so far been unknown. The draft provides that it will be the Minister of Justice who will be able to appoint the Presidents of the Appeals, District and District Courts on a one-man basis without the need to obtain an opinion on the candidates from the general assembly of these courts. Thus, the competition procedures are completely eliminated and the constitutional body of the National Judicial Council is excluded from this procedure. Moreover, the Minister of Justice will also be able to dismiss those functional judges in any and unfettered manner. This will enable him to very laconic and generalise the reasons for appeal in the law itself. At this point, it is worth recalling the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal of 9 November 1993, in which it was found to be incompatible with the principles of division of power and independence of the courts of the law, which granted the Minister the right to appoint and dismiss the governors of appeals and voivodeships after consulting the non-binding opinion of the general assembly, as well as the right to appoint and dismiss vice-presidents of appeal courts, voivodeships and the President and Vice-President of the regional court, excluding any, even non-binding, participation of the judiciary. In the context of these new powers of the Minister of Justice, it should also be stressed that the transitional provisions of the draft law provide for the possibility for the Minister to dismiss all current judges until the end of the year. What's important is that this can happen without giving any reason. Law and Justice, therefore, does not even create a pretense that this law is to sanction staff cleansing in Polish courts. If we look at the functioning of the Polish prosecutor's office, which Minister Ziobro has ruled completely for more than a year, what is the scale of purgatory and politicisation there, we may have the worst fear that a similar phenomenon will also affect the Polish judiciary. The current Constitutional Tribunal can undoubtedly be a threat. The right to refer to judges of the Tribunal who do not respect the previous judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal, who do not have any restraint to say that they represent the government and will defend it, let alone that the pace of work in the Court has fallen several times compared to previous years. The draft sets out very widely the situations in which the Minister will be able to press the judges, as it gives him the opportunity to request the President of the Court to remedy the deficiencies observed and will also be able to reward deserved judges with rapid promotions. The same powers shall be conferred on the President of the Republic of Poland in respect of judges of the Supreme Court. This solution is incompatible with Article. Here it is necessary to restore and repeat what I will say with stubbornness, Art. 4 constitutions: the sovereignty of the Republic of Poland is exercised by the nation either directly or by its representatives, which means that judges do not have the right to claim to choose themselves and to elect their own representatives. This is to be done by the nation either directly or by its representatives. We therefore propose, as a parliamentary support team, to consider this proposal for indirect elections by the people elected by the voivodship judicial councils, which would elect candidates subsequently presented to the National Judicial Council, which would further present this selected candidate to the President for approval. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u149	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to thank you, first of all, because the project, after working in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, maintains solutions to improve the functioning of the courts, improves the transparency of their actions and makes judges independent from the Presidents of the courts. In my view, this offers the chance for the courts to act more quickly and more effectively, so that citizens' confidence in justice gradually increases. The changes that are retained in the draft and which I welcome are important: the drawing of cases, the principle of equal responsibilities for judges, the indistinguishability of the formation of the judge, the limitation of the power of the governors in the transfer of judges, the full and public declarations of property of judges, clear and based on the substantive grounds of the principle of promotion, the possibility for women of a general age, the universal retirement age, i.e. 60 years, the system of modern court management, the reduction of judicial bureaucracy, the rationalisation of the system of visits, the assessment of the operation of courts, the improvement of the efficiency of the use of funds collected in the context of monetary benefits from the perpetrators of the offences on the justice fund, and the introduction of institutions of the coordinators. international cooperation, European law and human rights. The last of these changes is the delegation of judges to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland. But thank you not only for the fact that in this respect the project does not deviate from the assumptions set out in our proposal, but also for all the votes in the discussion and proposals for further changes. The opinions submitted and during the discussion raised doubts about some of the institutions and regulations of the project. As a result of discussions in the subcommittee and committees, some of these doubts have been eliminated or mitigated. This discussion helped me to formulate amendments which, with the agreement of the subcommittee and committees, had already been adopted, and to suggest changes which had been tabled by the Law and Justice Club and, I hope, will be given a positive opinion at the additional committee meeting. I believe that all these amendments make the project better and give a better chance to carry out the reform. We included among others. when dismissing the Presidents of the Courts, the opinion of the National Judicial Council, which is binding in a situation where 2/3 of the members of the National Courts are in favour. In this respect, it seems that we have come to a large extent in line with the expectations formulated, among others. during discussions in this Chamber and later during the work of the committee. We gave up a binding opinion of the Minister of Justice when we appointed the Visitors. We added the need for an opinion by the General Assembly on the activities of these courts before the Presidents of these courts submit annual information on the activities of those courts. We have clarified the guidelines for the internal rules of the general courts. We've clarified the rules on the doctors of the jurors, the writing of these letters, the indications. We have also clarified issues concerning the handling of cases by delegated judges. We have created a mechanism to control the decisions of the Presidents of the Courts in holding back the allocation of further cases. We have indicated the rationale for deciding to allow the judge to continue his post once he has reached his retirement age. Finally, we have excluded the possibility of delegation of powers to the Secretary of State or the Undersecretary of State in cases of transfer of a judge in the cases referred to in Article. 75 § 2. The last thing I want to point out is that we have made the rules of appointment more flexible for the prosecutor's office and we have made the rules more precise for the transfer of judges. In other words, a significant part of the regulation that was adopted during the work of the subcommittee and the committee followed the expectation to limit the possibility of indirectly influencing the independence of judges. Therefore, for some time in the Western countries, we have seen a tendency to see, however, that, in view of the fact that it is very sensitive data collected by courts, it is carried out under full or fuller control of public authority or even under public authority. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u165	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the Law on the System of General Courts and on certain other laws, prints Nos 1491 and 1591. As I mentioned in my first reading, this project is a long-awaited attempt to amend the law governing the general judicial system. There is nothing to hide that changes in the functioning of the justice system in Poland, and in particular in the functioning of the general court, are very necessary. In my speech today, I will address the issues that I put forward in my first speech, considering them crucial to the assessment of the project as a whole. First, the Act introduces the principle of random assignment of cases to judges and the principle of equal burden on judges in cases in order to ensure an equal and fair burden on the work of judges and to guarantee the impartiality of the parties to proceedings. That's a very good proposition. Currently, the requirement of random allocation applies only in criminal proceedings, and to a very limited extent. The other cases are assigned by the head of departments, and this is not always an assignment that ensures full transparency. Needless to say, the system allows abuse and does not build citizens' confidence in justice. The proposal was welcomed by many judges, in particular regional judges, who were often very burdened with work. The principle of random allocation of cases guarantees the parties access to the competent court. However, everything depends on the practical side of the implementation of this principle, namely the application of a special algorithm allocating the case within the ICT system. I'm asking the Minister to answer the questions. Has this algorithm been created yet? Are the relevant draft implementing acts and the draft IT programme ready to draw cases, ensuring that there is no possibility to influence the lottery draw? Secondly, the draft law adds an article. 47b, which introduces the principle of the invariability of the court. This proposal should also be assessed positively. At the moment, the unchangeable composition of the court ruling in civil proceedings is not regulated by law, and the practice in courts is subject to the orders of the Presidents of the courts or of the heads of departments who decide to assign cases to judges. As in the case of lots of cases, exceptions to this rule have also been introduced for situations such as: sickness or transfer of the judge. Thirdly, as with the issues discussed above, the committee adopted without major changes the model proposed in the draft law for the appointment of the Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Courts. The proposal contained in the proposal assumes that the Minister of Justice will appoint the Presidents of the Appeals and District Courts without having to obtain an opinion on the appointments for these posts. The amendment also provides for the appointment by the Minister of Regional Courts and not, as is currently the case, by the competent President of the Court of Appeal. The proposals adopted by the committee are moving towards increasing the independence of the Presidents of the courts vis-à-vis the hierarchised judicial environment, which we believe will positively affect the functioning of the justice system in Poland. Attention, though. In our view, discussion is included in the article. 27 Paragraph 1 (1) of the appeal procedure for the Presidents of the courts, since the grounds for appeal are inconsequential, allowing discretion in the decision of the Minister to appeal. This issue needs to be clarified by setting clear criteria. Regardless of the above, we stand in the position of referring to the article. 4 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, that the optimal solution would be to elect the Presidents of the courts in general elections from among the candidates of judges who meet the conditions set out in the Act. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the procedated bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u181	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law on the National Academic Exchange Agency. In particular, the contested bill establishes the National Academic Exchange Agency, which will pursue the objectives of the State in relation to the internationalisation of higher education and science, with the necessary resources. As part of the implementation of its mission, the Agency will be, among others, initiated and undertaken activities supporting international academic exchanges and the process of internationalisation of Polish universities and scientific units, as well as disseminating information about the Polish system of higher education and science. That's a very good solution. The establishment of an agency will make coordination a single institution. Current efforts to internationalise Polish science are scattered, which reduced the effectiveness of promoting the scientific achievements of Polish universities. Another problem arising from the current system is the lack of adaptation of the educational offer of our universities to the needs of students living in different geographical regions and the low level of funding for scholarships, which is not competitive with the financial assistance proposed by other European countries. The system also lacks one current information base on the scholarship offer for both outgoing and incoming students. The contested law facilitates the resolution of the problems presented. The activity of Poland in the international arena in disseminating scientific achievements or allowing the free use of foreign educational services is an important task and we should implement them not for the sake of the idea itself, but remembering that strengthening the quality of education builds the strength of our country. Currently, Poland has one of the lowest countries in the Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development levels of internationalisation of higher education. Polish scientists are not seen in international rankings, and the number of publications created in a foreign language leaves much to be desired. Low mobility of researchers, a small number of studies conducted in English and a small share of Poles in research projects of international rank make it necessary to make rapid but above all effective changes in the internationalization of Polish science. We also need to improve the employment rate of outstanding lecturers from abroad, which is what the proposed law is supposed to allow through, among others. negotiating salaries with them. The implementation, entry into force of the bill is intended to strengthen the position of Polish science in the world and indirectly contribute to the development of innovation in Poland, because the agency is to promote the Polish higher education system, implement a system of mobility programs and promote projects supporting universities in improving the quality of their educational offer. These actions in my opinion will bring tangible results in the form of viewing Poland as a dynamic academic centre in which it is worth studying. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni area requests that the law be further processed. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u203	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Code of Conduct on Offences in Sejm Printing No. 1562, amends the article. 98 of this law through - that is the main intention of this amendment - to enable the person punished to pay a mandate, a fine imposed by means of a criminal mandate using instruments to implement non-cash payments. The story connected with the implementation of this concept is quite long, because it dates back to the 2008-2009 years. Similar solutions were already planned and their main objective was to simplify the procedure of imposing fines on persons only temporarily residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland. That was the starting point. It was the first recipient in the original plan of this change. Police experience in this area pointed to a number of difficulties and complications related to the application of the mandate procedure, as it became increasingly common practice to use the argument used by foreigners, which was that they did not have any means of payment in Polish currency. Currently, in this form, which the government presents to the High House, including the draft law amending the Code of Conduct in cases of misconduct, the addressee of these records will be both persons temporarily residing in the territory of the Republic of Poland, on which fines are imposed, as well as persons who are permanently resident in the territory of Poland, on which mandates are imposed in a credit form. In the explanatory memorandum to the draft law, it is stated that Article. A new law would be added to the Code of Criminal Procedure. Thus, in the proposed § 3a it shall be possible to pay a fine imposed by means of a cash or credit mandate in non-cash form using a payment card or other payment instrument. The wording of the proposed rules indicates that the payment of a fine in a non-cash form will be a voluntary solution from which the fine can be used, provided that the fine-making officer has the appropriate means to authorise such settlements. The need for this condition is due to the fact that the equipment in question will be gradually put into service by the authorised services and institutions. In the proposed § 3b It has been established that the costs associated with the transfer of funds to the relevant bank account in the case of non-cash fines will be punished. In § 3c it is proposed to require an officer imposing a fine to inform the punished person of the possibility of paying a fine imposed in the form of a cash or credit mandate in a non-cash form and the costs involved. The proposed solution, as I said, is addressed to all entities entitled to impose fines... Sorry, I haven't said that yet. Now that's what I want to emphasize. This is addressed to all entities entitled to impose fines by means of criminal mandates, but this will mostly concern the police, because this formation is due to its size, scope of action, scope of powers granted and the obligations imposed reveals the highest number of offences. In 2015 alone, police officers revealed more than 8 million such acts, with more than 5.5 million cases of criminal offences being subject to a mandate procedure. I would like to stress very clearly that the solutions proposed are that a fined person in the form of a mandate will be able to pay a fine in this form, but will not have to, of course, be able to do so by his own choice if a police officer, if the police have the means to do so. These devices will be introduced in an evolutionary manner, so it will certainly not be possible anywhere immediately, but we assume that already in the first stage the police will be equipped with a number of payment terminals which will allow the use of them primarily by police patrols in traffic. It is these fines that could first be paid using a non-cash form, a payment penalty or other instruments. A policeman - I have already recalled this information - will be forced to inform the punished person of the possibility and conditions of paying the criminal mandate in this form. I would like to stress that these provisions are, on the one hand, seeking to simplify the procedures and actions relating to the payment of fines in the form of a criminal mandate, which is increasingly being applied today, i.e. in the form of non-cash trading, using a payment card or other instrument, so this solution undoubtedly responds to technological progress and social expectation, but on the other hand it will also reduce the risks associated with suspicions and corruption behaviours, and, more broadly, will certainly reduce the risk of the grey zone and pathologies in this area. In addition, it should be recalled that the proposed amendment is part of the programme under the name "From paper to digital Poland', which follows from the Resolution No 14/2016 adopted by the Council of Ministers of 16 February 2016 "Plan for responsible development'. So I think that this proposed, but also the expected social change should be accepted and accepted by all parliamentary clubs of the Polish Sejm, that is what I ask. I am convinced that it will also be well received by the public, by those who may be affected by these changes. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u229	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Way of Youth! The proposed law aims to restore confidence in the profession of bailiff as a public officer. That's the first target. And the second goal is to extend supervision of the bailiffs. The bailiff finally becomes a public servant, a public authority. We're restoring his rightful place. His place is in the country, not in the business. He is primarily to execute judgments on behalf of the Republic of Poland and is to apply coercive measures on behalf of the Republic of Poland. It is not from doing business, it is not from running your business and taking care of your business. He's called to the service, not to take care of his business, his business. We're clearing out the bailiffs. The execution fee will finally go to the state where it belongs and will not be the bailiff's salary. The bailiff will be paid, but for his service, his due service, his honest service, what we expect him to do. We're putting in clear statements of property. The bailiff isn't gonna be using assailants the way it's been. As far as detailed solutions are concerned, the proposed changes aim to introduce a model of small law firms, as I said earlier, in connection with the liquidation of these bailiff warehouses, and place emphasis on the personal execution of activities by the bailiff. The principle of personal performance of these activities is detailed in a number of solutions to this law, especially in the case of bailiffs. Asesura serves as a practical preparation for the profession, for the profession itself, not for helping the bailiff and proverbially carrying the briefcase behind the bailiff. In order to highlight the level of substantive knowledge of bailiffs, we accept the principle that the person performing this profession must be a lawyer, that is, we raise standards. The current rules have allowed bailiffs to pursue their profession by those who are not lawyers, which must be assessed negatively. We also raise the limit of the minimum age of the person appointed for this post from 26 to 28. This solution is modelled on solutions relating to the judge. At the same time, we reduce the upper limit of the age of the bailiff from 70 to 65 years, which is justified by the specificity of the work of the bailiff. It often requires physical immunity, physical condition, mental immunity, and therefore this decrease in age is necessary. Supervision of bailiffs is a multi-stage supervision: administrative, but also judic acid, supervisory and internal supervision of the bailiff's self-government. Solutions are proposed to increase the supervision of the Minister of Justice over the activities of bailiffs and bailiffs. At the same time, we define the precise rules of this supervision, we create an institution of supervision of the responsible President of the regional court. So, let's face it, we're moving away from the corporate model, we're restoring bailiffs to the state. The bailiffs are neither businessmen, nor court workers, nor persons who, at their own expense, conduct their own business on their own behalf. These are state officials and they are primarily responsible, both for costs and for service, and they are primarily responsible to you. In the project we introduce the principle that enforcement fees are to be charged to the state budget - as I mentioned - and the bailiff will receive a commission remuneration proportional to the amount of the fees collected. How has this worked in recent years? The execution fees in their entirety constituted the income of the bailiff running the firm. As a result, there has been a psychic situation: we have actually been dealing with the privatisation of public authority. Let us be clear that part of the public authority, part of the government has been privatized - in terms of charging, but not only. These fees should be a public tribute. They will not be able to conduct business activities of the National Chamber Council and other organs of the bailiff's self-government. At present, the local government can carry out such activities, which is critically assessed and with which we also end. The bailiffs, as I pointed out at the outset, are public officials and their activities cannot be directed towards maximizing profits. The bailiffs aren't businessmen, they're public officials. They should be in service, not in their private business. The proposed law also provides for the amendment of a number of laws, including: Code of civil procedure. We introduce police involvement in enforcement activities with the law of police intervention in the event of a finding of circumstances justifying suspicion of committing a crime, so that bailiffs have an armed arm of the state in a situation where there is a threat to their health or life. This change is also necessary to coordinate both the activities of the services - in this case the police - and the activities of the bailiffs. In order to improve the procedure, we also make a number of positive changes to the Code of Civil Procedure, which allow enforcement applications and complaints about bailiff activities on forms prepared by the Minister of Justice. The bailiff will record execution operations by means of devices that fix both the sound and the image. This also serves to introduce transparency, which also serves to introduce the principle of objectivity and transparency of these activities. At the same time, some evidence is being established in case the citizen has certain claims against the bailiff in the future. This evidence, collected in a transparent, clear and transparent manner, can be used if the bailiff, when performing his duties, exceeds his powers or fails to fulfil his duties. Finally, in the Law on the System of Universal Courts, we introduce the possibility of setting up enforcement departments in common courts, which will contribute to increasing the supervision of the Juditicians over the activities of bailiffs. These are specialized departments that will only serve this purpose. There will be judges who are prepared to perform this function. So far, we have had a situation that should also be assessed negatively: that is, the judge, in addition to performing his daily duties, had to step in at the same time into the judican supervision of the bailiffs - which distracted him from many other activities - which he did not perform on a daily basis and in which he was not specialized. In the name of the specialization of the justice system that we are betting on, we are introducing this solution. I ask the High House to adopt this positive draft law in order, as I mentioned, to restore respect for bailiffs and to introduce clear rules for supervision of bailiffs. In view of the above, please make a positive reference to this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u230	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u231	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the government draft law on bailiffs in print No. 1582. This project is a completely new draft law on bailiffs, repealing the previous law of 29 August 1997. The need for profound changes is due to many shortcomings and pathologies that we have dealt with regularly. We are fully aware that enforcement proceedings are, as it were, an extension, a continuation of judicial proceedings - and this is therefore a very important stage in the enforcement of the rights enshrined in court rulings. However, clear and transparent rules must apply here. First of all, it must be stressed that the bill retains the existing position of the bailiff, but it remains a public officer and is not an official included in the structure of the court. The project provides as a personal principle the execution of enforcement activities. So far, the bailiff may have used assesors in almost every way, so in many cases, when there were allegations of the correctness of his actions, the only person responsible was the asessor. Despite obtaining the economic equivalent of these activities, the bailiff often informally instructed the assessor and did not assume professional responsibility. The principle of the bailiff's personal action, in particular as regards field activities, is a guarantee of responsible action and within the limits of the law. In this respect, it is also worth pointing out that the law introduces restrictions - up to 60 days - on the replacements of the bailiff to which the court consents. Currently we are dealing with cases of substitutions lasting most of the year. Another proposed change is a region limiting the maximum area of action to the appeal district. This solution, which eliminates the uneven distribution of cases among bailiffs, reduces costly bailouts in remote areas of the country and significantly reduces the coincidence of executions. Another proposed change is to reduce the number of cases to 5 000, and in case of backlogs up to 2500. This will eliminate the so-called phenomenon. Chamber wholesalers, which by taking a very large number of cases made mistakes and errors, and the consequences of these errors and errors were not only the creditors and debtors, but often also third parties. The Act also strengthens the supervision and powers of the Minister of Justice through inter alia. a wide catalogue of grounds for suspension and cancellation of the bailiff, both mandatory and optional. The Act also introduces the disclosure of claims of property, which today is a standard among public officials. To a large extent, the Law related to civil proceedings contains the revised enforcement rules, which in most cases have been in force since 1 January 1965. This includes: Such cases, such as the repeal or suspension of immediate enforceability in a judgment by default, also concern the simplification of enforcement proceedings, enforcement applications forms, forms of complaints against the actions of the bailiff, the creation of a enforcement department in regional courts. It is all intended to improve the procedure so that judgments which are passed on behalf of the Republic of Poland are actually enforced. The Act shall enter into force on 1 January 2018, except that the provision of the Article. Point 260 (11), which concerns the preservation of bail-in operations by means of image and sound recording devices, will enter into force from 1 April 2018. The Law and Justice Club supports this presentation by the government and calls for it to be given a legislative run. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The bailiffs run very different cases, including those concerning the execution of alimony. In conclusion, this draft law contains a number of inconsistent and incomprehensible provisions. Its adoption in the shape proposed by the government, instead of harmonisation, can create chaos and confusion in the entire judicial execution system, which in fact makes it harder for the proverbial average Kowalski to investigate his debts. I think this is the main flaw of this bill, this draft proposed by the government, prepared by the Ministry of Justice. In our view, the project is falsely convinced that it solves any problems related to the functioning of the judicial enforcement system. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u235	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present our position, Kukiz Club15, regarding the draft law on bailiffs, print No. 1582. U. z 2006 r., poz. 1138, late. zm. Well, the bailiffs bill was amended almost 40 times, including five times very wide. The extent of the bailiff's attachment changed and its capabilities changed. However, all these changes led, unfortunately, to the loosening of the bailiffs, to the so-called formation of the so-called. bailiff factories, as well as for many common bailiff crimes. In our view, the current changes are moving in the right direction. A model of small bailiffs is proposed and emphasis is placed on the personal performance of bailiffs by the bailiff. The bailiff remains a public official and cannot conduct business activities, de facto restrictions are introduced such as for judges, maybe with the exception of scientific, educational and scientific work. The proposal restores the principle that enforcement fees are to be charged to the State budget and that the bailiff will receive a commission remuneration fixed in proportion to the fees collected, depending on the result of his work. The law does not prejudge whether the bailiff is a VAT taxable person or not, at least as a result of the justification of the project. The consequence of the change is the change in the current nature of the bailiff's remuneration. Enforcement fees, fixed interest rates, will be replaced by the so-called remuneration. commission: 99% with enforcement fees not exceeding 500 000. zł and 60% with enforcement fees exceeding PLN 2 million. In this case, our doubts are raised by the fact that the Treasury is earning money from debtors, especially since the bailiffs will no longer be self-employed, but will be public officials paid by public funds. It's a bit of a construction, I'll admit honestly, it's not known what, because once he's a public officer, once he's a businessman, once he does court orders, which is, let's say, the Treasury. The draft also introduces the principle of giving the parties written instruction about their rights. Participation in police enforcement activities shall be introduced with the possibility of blocking executions when there is a breach of the law. Video recording of bailouts is being introduced. On the other hand, in the event of a manifest violation of the law, the costs of lodging a complaint against the bailiff's activities will be borne by the bailiff himself and I will give an example in my position, which I will present when discussing the next draft law. The rules of execution from movable property shall be amended by specifying precisely the directory of non-occupied movable property. The bailiff will have to be a lawyer, which hasn't happened yet. The minimum age from 26 to 28 years of age is also increased and the upper limit of the age of the bailiff is limited to 70 to 65 years of age. The possibility for the Ministry of Justice to revoke the bailiff from office shall be introduced if the law is grossly and persistently infringed. And this raises the question: What is gross and persistent violation of the law? This is usually never defined and is the reason for great discretion. The rules for the training of applicants are also being amended and it is specified that the maximum duration of the assesura will not be longer than 6 years, while so far the assessor can perform his activities indefinitely. The President of the Court of Appeal will be responsible for this, and this provision will not apply to assesors who started assesura before the entry into force of the Act. The project also sets out issues related to disciplinary responsibility. The Disciplinary Ombudsman will be appointed by the Minister of Justice from among the three candidates presented by the National Chambers Council. Kukiz,15 will support the referral of this draft law to the Legislative Commission. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u269	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Way of Youth! As Minister Patryk Jak suggested, the draft bill on the costs of bailiffs, Parliamentary printing No. 1581, is in line with the previous project we discussed. It introduces above all a fixed fee - 10% the benefits enforced. At the moment we have two levels of fees: 15% and 8%. The current model leads to irregularities that are very common in cases where 8% are due fee, higher fee is required, 15%. So we need a clear and transparent charging system, a system, as the Minister called it, linear, just a linear fee system of 10%. At the same time, however, in carrying out the judgment of the Constitutional Court P 3/14, we wanted to introduce a 3% privilege the benefits enforced. Namely, the Court found that, if the debtor had fulfilled his performance even before he was served with a notice of initiation or a call for voluntary enforcement, the bailiff would return to the creditor part of the fee paid in excess of PLN 200. Therefore, if the debtor's voluntary performance before the date of entry into force of the obligation is fulfilled, it should be rewarded and we introduce this premium of 3%. Simple, fair costs easy to count - this is the main principle of this project. This law, like the one in the previous project, has been amended more than 40 times since 1997, including five times in a comprehensive manner. She's lost her inner cohesion. As a result, cost enforcement causes many problems. A huge number of complaints against bailiffs are about costs. This project is supported by a transparent and, above all, a simple way of financing the judicial enforcement system. And the arrangements to motivate debtors to pay their debts voluntarily are, of course, on the ground that they are, of course, the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Court which I mentioned. So in the project apart from what I was talking about, the introduction of a specific rope charge in the form of 10% , it is proposed to introduce a rule whereby the level of both minimum and maximum charges and fixed charges is determined in the form of a fraction of the average remuneration, rather than as yet. At the same time, we rationalise the amount of enforcement fees, indicating that the minimum fee is PLN 100 and the maximum amount must not exceed PLN 50 000. zł. As regards the next amendment, namely the application for the execution of the money claim security and the European Account Preservation Order, we propose to maintain the principle that the creditor shall bear such a fee at the stage of the application. In the event of the execution of cash collateral, which consists in an obligation to pay a single or periodic sum of money to the eligible person, e.g. maintenance claims, the rules on fees for the execution of cash benefits will apply accordingly. This is because the execution of such a guarantee is no different from the execution procedure. In section IV of the project we standardize the exemption from the costs of the bailiffs and the institution of the reduction of the fee. The exemption may result from the exemption granted to the party from legal costs - the exemption from legal costs entails exemption from bail-in costs, hence some consequence, it is logical - or exemption from bail-in costs granted by the court or referee. We introduce a regulation according to which the State Treasury will not be obliged to pay enforcement fees. We also provide clear grounds for exemption from bail-in costs, indicating that the possibility of applying for such exemption applies only to those costs on which the bailiff's action depends. However, the exemption of the parties from the bail-in costs does not exempt them from the obligation to pay the enforcement fee for the execution of cash benefits and to reimburse the costs of enforcement proceedings to the other party where there is a legal basis for such a charge. The district court in which the bailiff operates shall pay the bailiff temporarily the sum corresponding to the amount of expenses and charges on which the bailiff's action depends. The institution of sizing - application for reduction - enforcement fee will continue to refer only to fees for the execution of cash benefits. It currently exists and the proposed provisions do not change its nature. We maintain the existing conditions for a reduction in the fee associated with the work of the bailiff and the property situation of the party required to pay the fee. We expressly provide for exemption from court fees in respect of the application for reduction of the enforcement fee, as well as complaints and complaints against the referee's rulings in these cases. Finally, in section V of the project we include amending, transitional and final provisions. They relate to the Code of Civil Procedure in the context of the regulation of the costs of enforcement proceedings and the Law on Judicial Costs in respect of the charge of the complaint against the actions of the bailiff and other court fees in enforcement proceedings. However, all proceedings shall be governed by the rules on fees levied in the event of redemption of enforcement proceedings at the creditor's request or as a result of his inactivity. The Council of Ministers adopted a draft law on 16 May this year. Following the transfer of the project to parliamentary work, no new comments were received. In view of the above, I ask the High House to accept this proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u270	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u271	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the government proposal for a bill on bail-out costs. This project is a natural consequence of the reform of the bailiff's profession, as well as a new regulation on the costs of bailiffs. Let me remind you that there are separate regulations regarding judicial costs in civil matters and judicial costs in criminal matters. Not so long ago, because at the beginning of the 90s of the last century, the bailiff's costs were governed by a regulation of the Minister of Justice. The bailiff's expenses and fees are elements of the enforcement procedure, which in many cases decide whether or not to pursue the enforcement procedure. The project assumes a basic fee of 10% compared to today's two basic rates: 8% and 15% . Uniformity will undoubtedly simplify the procedure and statistically reduce it. It will also eliminate the selection of enforcement measures dictated by the amount of the enforcement fee. The rate of 5% should also be very positive. in the case of voluntary payment to the account and 3% in the case of payment within 14 days. This is a great incentive for voluntary fulfillment of the service. The existing provisions will apply to proceedings initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act, subject to Article. 28 and 29. The Act will enter into force on 1 January 2018. Although the proposed changes should be positively assessed, in particular the reduction of the fee, the calculation requires that small firms in small towns, where there are long-distance departments, e.g. Just the civilian department or the perpetual books department, where there's one bailiff's office, or are these little bailiff's offices really gonna hold up? I know that in the discussion in the previous item, also in the statements of representatives of individual clubs and in the questions, the elements, questions which concerned precisely this law, also in the speech of Mr Patrick Jaki's Vice Minister of Justice. The amount of 45 thousand given by him. as an average of the earnings of the bailiffs, it is probably taken from those wholesalers, where there are revenues of more than PLN 5 million per year.
