ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u286	Mr Krzysztof Paszyk, Polish People's Party, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u287	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party a position on the government draft bill on bail-out costs contained in print No. 1581. The aim of the proposed law is to introduce transparent rules for the financing of the judicial enforcement system and arrangements to motivate debtors to voluntarily repay the obligations identified by enforcement titles. The project also sets out the amount of the bail-in costs and the rules for carrying them, as well as the procedure for dealing with them. According to the project, the bail-in costs include the expenses of the bailiff in the course of the proceedings and the fees of the bailiffs. These will include enforcement fees and fees for other proceedings or other actions. Basically, the enforcement procedure and other proceedings conducted by the bailiff, e.g. for the purpose of drawing up an inventory, as well as other activities commissioned by the bailiff, which are not executional activities, e.g. service of letters. This means that the traditional allocation of costs into fees and expenses has been maintained. It is maintained that the final provision on the costs due to the bailiff is enforceable without a declaration of enforceability. However, the new solution is to indicate that no enforcement fee will be charged for the execution of bail-in costs. This will eliminate situations where the provisions on costs due are the basis for the initiation of separate enforcement proceedings, for an additional fee, before a bailiff other than the one who issued the order. It is undoubtedly, High Izbo, a good direction. I think that here - at least in most - we agree that it is not necessary to question the need to adopt a separate bill on the costs of bailiffs in order to put this matter in order, especially in view of the simultaneous preparation of a separate bill regulating the system and the rules governing the pursuit of the profession of bailiffs. It is undoubtedly appropriate to gather and organise the subject matter, including the issues that have emerged in connection with the practice of the last 20 years. The draft law on bail-out costs rationalises enforcement fees. The current profitability of bailiffs remains at odds with earnings in other legal professions. However, it is appropriate to agree with the President, Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz, that it is relatively easy to fire a child with a bath on this issue. I, from the other end of Poland, from Wielkopolska, would also be able to point out at least a few examples, such as those indicated by Mr Matusiewicz, that this could lead to the fact that today's existing law firm will cease to exist in a moment, which could condemn potentially seeking help in this law firm to travel considerable distances in order to turn its affairs around. However, it should be noted that many firms, as we have said here, differ from this average in terms of profitability. As the bailiffs themselves are alarmed, this bill could lead to the collapse of many law firms. The authors of the bill in the explanatory memorandum wrote about bailiffs earning millions of zlotys. Well, it's actually worth it here, Minister, but watch out for the use of such arguments. Recently, the National Council of Komornicza presented a report prepared at its request on the basis of surveys conducted among bailiffs from all over Poland. According to this report, almost 60% of chamber firms will record a zero or negative result after the changes have been made. According to the report, operating difficulties will result in serious delays in recovery of outstanding debts, which could lead to negative economic and social consequences. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The crisis in the industry can also affect young people who are starting to learn in this profession. These are, of course, certain shortcomings. They should not obscure the essential objective...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u317	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present to you a draft law amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and some other laws. The aim of the bill is to introduce pension allowances for those who are eligible for voluntary firefighters' activities. The project provides for the grant of a pension allowance to persons who have been active members of the voluntary fire department for at least 20 years. Let me repeat: the project concerns only active members of the voluntary fire department. In the course of consultations with volunteer firefighters, we have established that active membership of voluntary firefighters means a membership lasting a total of at least 20 years, during which the insured took part in at least half of the average municipal number of fire-fighting and rescue operations. The CSR partners are volunteers and professionals at the same time. They are required to be fit and healthy, as confirmed by appropriate tests. Firefighters have very good tactical and combat training, which involves a great amount of time, with a significant psychological and physical burden. Volunteer firefighters are an organization which plays a particularly important role in rural areas, where the network of professional units of the State Fire Service is underdeveloped. Although voluntary fire workers gather on a voluntary basis, it is worth noting that they are very well-trained, professionally organized and strongly involved in fulfilling their tasks. Volunteer firefighters are a very important element of public service, under which citizens are trained in discipline and deepen their sense of duty and dedication. In accordance with the Fire Protection Act, a member of the voluntary fire department who has participated in rescue or fire training organised by the State Fire Department or the municipality shall receive a cash equivalent for each hour of participation in rescue or training. The amount of the equivalent shall be determined by the municipality. The upper limit is 1/175 of the average monthly salary announced by the president of the CSO, i.e. slightly above 20 PLN. I would like to make it clear to you how much volunteer firefighters actually receive in one hour's time to participate. I have prepared several resolutions of the municipalities' councils, which are entitled to determine the amount of the equivalent. Very often these amounts are in the order of PLN 12.13 and PLN 4.6 for fire training. And so we have in official journals: Council of the Municipality of Paprotnia - 13 zł and 5 zł for each full hour of training, Ćmielów - 10 zł, commune of Rudka - 12 zł. And so you can go on and on. The amount of the proposed rates is so ridiculously low that the equivalent in part of the municipality is only functional formally. Often firefighters consciously refuse to receive it. This is because the municipalities have one pool of money to maintain the readiness of the volunteer fire department, from which the equivalent for firefighters is also paid. The bridesmaids are aware that by taking the equivalent, they reduce the fire budget of the municipalities, so they often renounce their resources to their own unit. The current state of regulation, which appreciates the contribution, involvement and work of voluntary firefighters, is highly unsatisfactory. The service provided by volunteer firefighters involves not only participation in actions, but also the need for regular physical preparation and the need for a large amount of time for CSR activities. In addition, the voluntary fire service involves a series of responsibilities and a full willingness to take action, which requires great discipline and a significant level of availability. It should be stressed that voluntary firefighters are not merely involved in fire rescue. Volunteer firefighters are also active in other fields, such as environmental protection, sport, education and education or culture. Volunteer firefighters carry out local activities to protect the natural environment, forests, nature reserves, national parks, urban gardens and natural resources. Many individuals conduct physical and sports culture classes, organize sports competitions and conduct sports facilities, as well as prepare tourist and recreational events. Many of them also engage in hobby activities, running clubs and circles of interest, including various educational and educational activities. In the field of culture and art, voluntary firefighters are also very active in organizing local cultural events: holidays, festivals or festivals that support tradition and folk culture. The firefighter shall not be entitled to any cash benefits for such activities, carried out outside rescue operations or trainings. The involvement of volunteer firefighters in the ministry is admirable and involves a heavy psychological and physical burden. Therefore, in the opinion of the projectors, the introduction of an allowance to the pension for members of voluntary fire services of PLN 20 per year of active service will only be a symbolic expression of respect and recognition for their dedicated service. The introduction of an allowance to the pension for persons serving at the TSO will result in a mission that they carry out not only as an honorary activity. The pension allowance is intended to in some way compensate them for their long-term dedication and activities in favour of local communities. In conclusion, I would like to stress once again that the role of volunteer firefighters who devote their spare time to others, often putting their lives and health at risk to save victims of various kinds of accidents, disasters or natural disasters, is beyond overestimation. As a result, firefighters should be given due respect and gratitude to their citizens. This gratitude should also be expressed in the form of financial support for a heavy, multi-annual, active service for the security of citizens and the protection of the country. To conclude, I would like to ask the High House to draw attention to the underappreciated CSR firefighters who, for the sake of merit to society as a whole, should, at least to a minimum, be rewarded. Please refer this draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy and the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u321	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It has been my honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and certain other laws, print Nos 852 and 852-A, and the citizens' bill amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and some other laws, print No 1534. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Volunteer firefighters deserve the utmost recognition. Without volunteer firefighters, it's hard to imagine the operation of a rescue system. Volunteers not only go to fires, rescue natural disasters, but also help victims of road accidents. Therefore, firefighters have enjoyed the greatest social trust for many years. The Polish government supports the activities of voluntary firefighters. We have introduced new rules to subsidise individuals in order to spend more rationally the funds, and the government of Prime Minister Beata Szydło increases the funds for TSOs, especially for individual volunteer firefighters and rescue equipment. This year these measures have been increased by several percent compared to last year. The draft laws discussed in this section aim to introduce pension allowances for those who are entitled to voluntary firefighters' activities. The rules for the payment of these allowances are set out here by the applicants. However, when discussing the projects submitted, it is necessary to note that since 1999 the social security system has been based on the principle of the equivalence of the contributions paid and the benefit due. The benefits paid under the general scheme are individual in nature and are determined on the basis of the contribution paid in the form of contributions. By reforming the pension system in 1998, the authority of that time emphasized that as much as you put together, you will receive in the future. This was indicated as the highest value and so in Poland the social security system and the pension system were constructed. It should be noted that the old-fashioned so-called general pension scheme has been abolished. The amount of assigned revenue in accordance with Article 21 (3) of the Financial Regulation is estimated at EUR 10000000. The abolition of the right to these allowances was due to the restoration of the pension system of its insurance character, which was finally determined by the reform of the social security system introduced in 1999. In the new pension scheme, defined by the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, the source of funding for pension benefits is the fund created by contributions from employees and employers. Most of the non-insurance and social components have been removed from the scheme in order to link the amount of the future benefit to the sum of the contributions paid from the insured's income during the entire period of his professional activity. Currently, only two types of allowances are paid under the Social Insurance Fund Act: the care allowance and the supplement for complete orphans. Both of these allowances are insurance benefits related to insurance risks in the form of incapacity for work and self-existence and loss of the host. The payment of these allowances shall take place in the event of the specified risks being met and shall entail the obligation to pay the insurance premium. Service in voluntary fire department units does not fall within the categories of insurance risks provided for by the social security system, which include, in particular, the loss of working income due to old age or incapacity to work due to health, while the Fire Protection Act provides for voluntary fire workers, inter alia. one-off compensation in the event of permanent or long-term injury or pension for total or partial incapacity for work. I would like to point out that pensions from pension provision are also paid in Poland, i.e. not from the Social Insurance Fund, but these are completely different legal solutions, other regulations. This is not the case for those laws whose amendment is proposed. On behalf of my Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to ask both of these projects to go further. [[♪ The bell ♪]] procedures, but in the joint committees: the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs and the committee that is appropriate for firefighters, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u328	Thank you. Now he's gonna take the floor... I understand there's not much time left. Mr Norbert Kaczmarczyk, just a few seconds. No. Mrs Monika Rosa, Modern Club. Member, please.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u329	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The involvement and sacrifice of those associated with TSOs should be appreciated by the public authority, including the financial dimension, and the way in which this is to be achieved requires an in-depth analysis. We are in favour of directing these projects to read in committee, but I would like to put forward a few questions that emerged after reading both projects. First of all, the pension system in Poland is so designed that it consists of a defined contribution, and the pension depends directly on the amount of contributions paid. So there's no room for that kind of accessory. Since the introduction of the pension reform, the only form of supplement is the care supplement for people unable to live independently and after over 75 years of age, and in the family pension system, the supplement for complete orphans. All these contributions, these benefits are paid by social security contributions. The social security system carries out its essential functions, protecting against loss of funds due to old age, illness, incapacity for work, and lacks room to carry out tasks consisting in rewarding or honoring anyone, also for sacrificing your time and health for the public good. Therefore, the Fire Protection Act is perhaps the appropriate place to compensate for participation in rescue operations. In the case of these draft laws, the designer goes beyond the applicable pension system, which is also underlined by the Supreme Court's opinion. If we were to contribute to the amount that volunteers are currently receiving, it would increase the operating costs of the TSOs within the municipalities, but would also not produce the expected results. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u335	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the citizens' and Members' proposals for amending the Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and certain other laws. Both the civil and the parliamentary project provides for the granting of an allowance to members of the voluntary fire department for retirement. In the case of the parliamentary project, additional funds of PLN 20 for each documented year of service would be paid to members of the voluntary fire department who have been active in service for 20 years. They would be volunteers who participated in at least half of their unit's shares. In turn, the citizens' project assumes that this supplement, at the same height as in the draft of the members of the Kukiz club15, would receive volunteers from the voluntary fire department who have participated directly in rescue operations for at least 10 years. There is no doubt that the voluntary fire department is an important element not only in our fire safety but also in our social and cultural life. Involving its members in the ministry and helping us requires proper recognition. However, I have some doubts about issues which, in my opinion, may affect the negative social perception of the proposed solutions and contribute to the emergence of conflicts. My skepticism arouses, among other things. the factor proposed in the parliamentary draft to ensure that the member concerned receives the allowance to the pension, i.e. the need for him to participate in at least half of his unit's shares. We know very well that depending on the municipality there are large discrepancies in the number of trips of the team to the event. This means that e.g. members of an individual from one municipality would be entitled to such an allowance if they had already participated in 10 shares, while members of a team from another municipality would have had to take these trips 50. Already now, by briefly reviewing the Internet forums on which the main stakeholders in the implementation of these regulations are concerned, it can be noted that this issue is becoming a subject of dispute and intense discussion. Doubts also arise if we think about the social character of the service in the voluntary fire department. Despite the money equivalent received due to participation in shares or training organised by the PSP or the municipality, membership of the TSO was primarily linked to the willingness to provide assistance to others. The introduction of the proposed solutions could derail this idea, leading to a situation in which voluntary activity will only be brought into a desire to obtain financial benefits. To sum up, both projects raise a number of considerations that would be worth discussing more. Apart from the reservations regarding the systemic location of the proposed regulations, it would be appropriate to consider what the impact on the state budget would actually have on the adoption of one of the solutions, taking into account also the fact that its introduction could result in similar claims from other, similar to TSOs, social organisations. The Free and Solidarnia Circle, bearing in mind the importance of supporting the activities of the State Fire Service, considers that both projects should be directed to further work in the committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

They differ in details concerning, in particular, the length of the service's active period, which entitles such an allowance in accordance with the draft law. As regards the reference to the intentions of the designers, I am talking here about both draft laws, the Council of Ministers is expressing a positive opinion in this regard. There is no doubt that the special sacrifice of those associated with voluntary firefighters should be noticed by the public authority, including the financial dimension. On the other hand, with regard to the way in which we agree with the designers, it requires an in-depth analysis. It can be said that with regard to the powers of the members of the voluntary fire department, these issues have so far been regulated by the Fire Protection Act. These are, in particular, rights in respect of the equivalent of participation in rescue or fire training, namely the right to one-off compensation in the event of permanent or long-term injury, i.e. entitlement to a pension for total or partial incapacity for work, as well as compensation for damage to property, where a member of the voluntary fire department in connection with participation in rescue operations, exercises have suffered injury or injury to property, i.e. the right to monetary compensation for each day of incapacity for work of 1/30 minimum wage, the right to special clothing and personal equipment, the right to periodic free medical examinations. As regards the extension of this catalogue as proposed by the projectors, the Council of Ministers expressed a negative opinion in this regard. This is a negative opinion with regard to the substantive solutions proposed in both projects, not with regard to intentions, which I stress. The motives for the position of the Council of Ministers appeared in the discussion on both draft laws. These recitals mainly referred to the assumptions concerning our pension system, because this element regarding appreciation, appreciation of the sacrificial service of voluntary firefighters, the projectors propose to implement precisely through changes in the pension system. This was also the issue of Mrs Tomaszewska's question as to whether we can imagine another way of achieving those intentions which we agree on. The principle of contribution, one can say, since 1 January 1999 is one of the main principles on which our pension system is based. In short, the right to benefit, but also the dimension of this benefit, is inextricably linked to the contribution, to the contribution obligation, and those proposals which are contained in both draft laws do not take into account this element. Service in voluntary fire department units does not fall within the categories of insurance risks provided for by the social security system, which does not mean, of course, that these possible damage in the form of e.g. Incapacity for work cannot be compensated by the social security system. The assumption of the social security system is that the scheme compensates by providing income lost as a result of a specific random event such as old age or incapacity to work. In the course of the discussion, there were also those arguments which are contained in the official position of the Council of Ministers, which relate to the need, in general, to respect the constitutional principle of equal treatment or equal treatment before the law. Namely, we note in our position that the designers did not include in the project people who are in the so-called. Relevance situation, i.e. those who are also rescuers, volunteers of the Water Volunteer Emergency Service, Mountain Volunteer Emergency Service or e.g. Tatra Volunteer Emergency. Our proposal contains even more detailed comments, already of a legislative nature, which I would not like to present to you at this stage in detail. Despite this negative substantive position of the Council of Ministers, I would like to say that the Council of Ministers supports the proposal to direct both projects to work in committee. During this work, I will present the position of the Council of Ministers in more detail. Thank you.

In point 3 of the preliminary request for constitutional liability of Jarosław Kaczyński, the charge of committing the offence described in the article was made. Article 231 (1) of the Criminal Code. The description of the prohibited act was formulated as follows: on 26 February 2007 and 27 March 2007 in the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers, he failed to fulfil his duties as President of the Council of Ministers in such a way that he initiated and participated in informal meetings with officials under the authority of the Minister of Justice to the Prosecutor General, etc. He didn't do his job because he took part in the meeting of the band he called. In fact, one of these teams met - for your information - once or twice and the other never met at all. [[Impossible.]] At the outset of the above plea, it is necessary to emphasise that for the purpose of assigning an offence under this article, which I mentioned earlier, it is necessary to state that the conduct of the perpetrator is carried out by a set of marks of a prohibited act set out in that provision. These are e.g. the following indications: it is an individual offence; it can only be committed by a public official; it is necessary to establish that a public officer has exceeded his or her powers or failed to fulfil his or her duties; by exceeding his or her authority or failure to fulfil his duties, the officer has acted to the detriment of the public or private interest; the act of the perpetrator must be a deliberate act, so that the perpetrator must include the awareness and intent of all the marks of the prohibited act and, at the same time, its effect; the act of the perpetrator must have an effect in the form of, for example, the occurrence of a danger of injury in the public or private interest. Each of the characteristics described above must be proven by the perpetrator in the course of criminal proceedings. The lack of implementation of at least one of these conditions justifies the fact that the conduct under criminal review is not a criminal offence. In view of the first of the elements of the prohibited act referred to above, it should be pointed out that the definition of the concept of "public officer" is laid down in Article. 115 § 13 of the Criminal Code. On the basis of the present case, there is no doubt that Jarosław Kaczyński served as President of the Council of Ministers at the time specified in the plea and was therefore a public officer. With regard to the mark in the form of excess of entitlements and the failure to fulfil their obligations, it is necessary for them to be careless and to have a significant degree of generality. According to the criminal law doctrine, the excess of powers means that the act of the perpetrator either did not fall within his or her competence at all, or was, indeed, within that scope, but was taken contrary to the law or other regulations governing the duties of a public officer. Failure to comply with obligations implies that the obligation imposed on that public officer is not taken into account by law. Therefore, the defence officer of the requested person is right to point out that, in the present case, the description of the prohibited act, with negative regard for certain behaviours, the actions of the former President of the Council of Ministers, misplaced them as failure to fulfil their duties. This form of crime concerns primarily the failure to take up or carry out obligations and not their defective performance. As indicated in the literature, the act of committing the crime of art. 231 § 1 of the Penal Code, although specified as an act to the detriment of the public or private interest, it may consist in action, overstepping or failing to fulfil its obligations. For a detailed reconstruction of the scope of the criminal law standard, you will get to the report and read there. Marks of non-compliance - you will read this already. And so: the provision of the article. 148 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland lists the basic tasks of the President of the Council of Ministers in carrying out the tasks of the Council of Ministers, namely representing the Council of Ministers, directing the work of the Council of Ministers, issuing regulations, ensuring the implementation of the policy of the Council of Ministers, determining the ways in which it is implemented, coordinating and controlling the work of the members of the Council of Ministers, exercising supervision over the territorial self-government within the limits and forms laid down in the Constitution and in the laws, is the official head of government staff. On the other hand, according to the article. The President of the Council of Ministers may, in particular, designate the Minister for the matters in which he acts under the authority of the President of the Council of Ministers, request information, documents and periodic reports, or relating to a particular case or matter, from the Minister, the Head of the Central Office, the voivodeships, etc., order the carrying out of correspondence arrangements between the members of the Council of Ministers, convene, take part in and chair the meetings of the auxiliary bodies of the Council of Ministers or the President of the Council of Ministers, irrespective of their composition and scope, convene meetings with and chair the relevant ministers, heads of central offices or voivodeships, either from the office or at the request of the competent authority or at the request of the party, a matter falling within the competence of more than one minister or head of the central office to deal with the minister designated by him, notifying all this to the other competent authorities and parties. Last but not least, determine the scope of the ministers' action in the event of a dispute between ministers. Mark and remind: Art. 148 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]] I'm not done yet. I'm done. Knowing the basic constitutional and statutory responsibilities, the powers of the President of the Council of Ministers cannot in any way be found to exceed the powers or, even more so, not to fulfil the duties of the President of the Council of Ministers by initiating and taking part in any meetings involving other officers subordinate to him and other members of the Council of Ministers. I'm appealing to the Constitution. Take a look at this. There is no doubt, however, that the broad powers of the President of the Council of Ministers in determining the direction of State affairs and in carrying out the duties contained in the law entitle him to meet in formal advisory bodies, as well as ad hoc, informal groups with subordinate government officials and representatives of other public authorities, including representatives of the President's office, local authorities, prosecutors, professional authorities or non-governmental organisations, associations, religious associations. The practice of the office of the President of the Council of Ministers shows that holding such meetings is an integral part of the duties arising from the exercise of this function. So, summing up, as you can see, everything was done in accordance with the Constitution, in accordance with Polish law. The proposals have no legal basis. This was assessed by the Committee on Constitutional Responsibility. If you would like to read this in detail, because what I have said is just such a general quote of the report as you want to read the report in detail, I invite you because it is very well prepared, substantive. ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u448	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on this report by the Committee on Constitutional Responsibility. First of all, it should be noted that the proposal was the subject of proceedings of the VII-term Sejm, which had not been completed before the end of that term and that, in accordance with the Law on the Court of State, is still pending during the VIII-term of the Sejm. The committee's report concerns the preliminary proposal of 19 November 2012 signed by 148 Members of the previous term of office. The applicant was represented by Mr Jerzy Kozron. The proposal contains allegations of what Jarosław Kaczyński was supposed to do during his term of office, acting as President of the Council of Ministers. The applicants argue that these are allegations eligible for constitutional responsibility of the former President of the Council of Ministers. Direct charges include: provisions which, according to the applicants, were intended to prevent the political and social activities of political opponents of the parties Law and Justice. Jarosław Kaczyński, acting President of the Council of Ministers, issued Order No 138 on 11 September 2006 on the Interministerial Team for the Prevention of Organised Crime and then, on 19 April 2007, Order No 40 on the Interministerial Team for the Prevention of Criminal Crime. The applicants stress that the aim of the two teams was to try to demonstrate that in the political, social and economic life of our country there is a supposed network of political and business-social links of a criminal nature, i.e. so-called. A deal. In addition, those regulations gave the Minister of Justice - the Prosecutor General - the authority over the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, the Chief of Police, the Head of the CBA and the Head of the ABW - to ask for information on the actions taken and to issue binding instructions to these authorities. This constitutes an infringement of Article. 2, Article 7 and Article 7 149 Constitution of the Republic of Poland, as well as art. 34 Act on the Council of Ministers and Art. 36 Government Departments Act. Moreover, on 26 February and 27 March 2007, he failed to fulfil his duties as President of the Council of Ministers in such a way that he initiated and participated in informal meetings with public officials under the Minister of Justice, the prosecutor general, at which meetings took place procedural decisions in cases conducted by the prosecutor's office, without, of course, prosecutors conducting preparatory proceedings in these cases. Thus, the provisions of the Act on the Prosecutor's Office, the Code of Criminal Procedure and the Act on the Council of Ministers were violated. This was evidently detrimental to the public interest and the regularity of the criminal proceedings. In conclusion, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party does not share the position of the Committee on Constitutional Responsibility, which, by the majority of the members of the Law and Justice, calls for the termination of this procedure. On the basis of the collected material and the conduct of the hearing of some witnesses, there are still many doubts and questions. One of them: Why did the applicant, Mr Jerzy Kozron, who is no longer present today, not take part in the committee's work, although we have sought to do so? Why is there so much doubt and the Barbara Blida case not fully resolved? Jarosław Kaczyński's actions are controversial. It clearly affected the interests of the state authorities, in particular the prosecutor's office, because decisions on procedural actions were made at political level and were not the result of the proceedings conducted. [[♪ The bell ♪]] By the prosecutors. We believe that the clarification of these many controversial cases before the Court of State is not a judgment for the former President of the Council of Ministers.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u508	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report to the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Bond Act, print No. 1586, and this report is print No. 1599. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Public Finance for first reading on 31 May 2017. The Bond Act already contains appropriate exemptions for the issue of bonds in order to qualify them as components of banks' own funds and investment firms. The provisions of the Bond Act should be supplemented by equivalent exemptions for insurance and reinsurance undertakings. The proposed amendments will allow insurance and reinsurance undertakings concerned to issue subordinated bonds to increase their own funds to cover capital requirements and market development. The project shall include favourable arrangements for insurance and reinsurance undertakings enabling them to issue subordinated bonds with the characteristics set out in the Delegated Regulation. The Public Finance Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 6 June 2017, brings forward: The High Sejm will approve the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u509	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Barbara Bubula, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u510	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on printing No 1586 and the resulting printing of the report of the committee, i.e. against the government draft law amending the law on bonds. The Act concerns a small segment of activities in the field of finance and insurance. As we learned from the presentation of the draft at the committee meeting, only about 160 economic operators are involved. It is a fair level playing field between banks and capital market players, which, in the current rules when issuing subordinated debt in the form of bonds, have the possibility to increase their own funds covering capital requirements and enabling market development. However, insurance and reinsurance undertakings did not have such an authorisation under the Bond Act. The draft law therefore aims to equip insurance and reinsurance undertakings with similar powers. It is therefore an instrument of equalisation of banking and capital market opportunities vis-à-vis the insurance market. These solutions are beneficial for entrepreneurs and in the longer term - as we hope - will also be beneficial for the clients of the insurance market. The Act therefore removes inconsistencies between the provisions of the Act of 11 September 2015 on Insurance and Insurance Activities and the European Directive known under the heading "Solvency II' and the Bond Act. Although the first two legal acts provide the possibility for insurance and reinsurance undertakings to include subordinated liabilities in the form of bonds as own funds, in practice, they have not been able to issue bonds in a manner that meets the requirements laid down in the Delegated Regulation, i.e. the European Commission Regulation 2015/35 of 10 October 2014. The removal of this inconsistencies will give the possibility to issue both subordinated bonds and perpetual bonds. The shortest possible vacathio legis proposed by the government in this law also does not raise any objections, as this is a solution for those interested in this change of legislation. In conclusion, the amendment proposed by the government to the Bond Act constitutes a necessary level playing field, facilitating the pursuit of business in the insurance and reinsurance market, and therefore further evidence of good changes in the rules governing the economy. Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill proposed by the government. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u511	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mrs Bożena Szydłowska, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-06-07-sejm-43-1.u512	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Bond Act, print no. 1586. The government bill amending the Bond Act is dedicated to 162 entities. 161 are insurance undertakings and one is reinsurance undertakings. The project provides for the possibility for these entities to include the issue of bonds in their own funds through the introduction of an appropriate exemption in the Bond Act. The proposed provisions are intended to allow insurance and reinsurance undertakings concerned to issue subordinated bonds to increase their own resources to cover capital requirements and market development of those entities. In the current legal state, insurance and reinsurance undertakings are not in a position to issue subordinated debt in the form of bonds in a manner which at the same time meets the requirements of the Bond Act and the requirements resulting from the provisions for basic own funds laid down in the Delegated Regulation. There is a conflict between EU and national legislation. This is related to the article. 74. 2 The Bond Act, which shows that the redemption does not have an option for the issuer and that the redemption may take place within a shorter period than that reserved by the Delegated Regulation. The same effects on insurance market operators are affected by Article. Article 75 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 2 of the Act on Perpetual Bonds. In order to qualify subordinated or perpetual bonds as fixed assets, insurance and reinsurance undertakings will classify issued bonds in the first basic own funds category or in the second basic own funds category, depending on the conditions of the Delegated Regulation. The proposed shortening of vacathio legis will not have negative effects on insurance and reinsurance undertakings. High Izbo! The above-mentioned specialised establishments are awaiting the elimination of the collision and equal treatment of banking, capital and insurance entities. Therefore, my Civic Platform club is in favour of directing the project to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention.
